```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	As language models increasingly train on web text containing their own outputs, a recursive model$\to$data$\to$model loop arises, yet no method estimates how many such iterations a text has undergone.
3	We formalize \emph{recursive generation depth estimation} as ordinal classification, constructing a 180K-passage benchmark spanning 3 model families, 3 decoding strategies, and 4 depths under controlled full-replacement recursive chains at the 1--1.5B scale.
4	A Random Forest over 19 statistical features, dominated by the perplexity tail family (\texttt{p90\_ppl}, \texttt{var\_surprisal}), achieves 69--86\% four-class accuracy (d1--d3 balanced: 59--82\%; chance = 25\%/33\%); the convergence of RF, XGBoost, and LightGBM within 0.4\,pp confirms that discriminative signal resides in feature design, not classifier choice.
5	Cross-model transfer reaches 66.4\% four-class (d1--d3: 55.4\%), cross-domain arXiv 74.1\%, and a single-cell prompted-generation test 69.5\%.
6	Cross-strategy transfer (59.1\%, d1--d3: 45.9\%) reveals that decoding strategies induce structurally distinct degradation trajectories, a finding we formalize via the \emph{Effective Discriminable Depth} (EDD) metric ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}4$ for all three families).
7	Preliminary cross-scale experiments (7--14B, 54--70\%) provide an existence proof that depth signal persists at larger scales, though confounded by five factors including quantization and vocabulary mismatch.
8	Binary detectors and membership inference baselines (30--35\%) fall ${>}$43\,pp below our pipeline, confirming that ordinal depth estimation requires distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text.
9	\end{abstract}
10	
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	This paper formalizes recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification under controlled full-replacement chains.
5	The perplexity tail feature family dominates depth discrimination across all nine cells, with $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}4$ for all three families (confirmed by 5-class extension, 71--80\%; Appendix~\ref{app:depth4_ext}); cross-model transfer reaches 66.4\% (d1--d3: 55.4\%), cross-domain arXiv 74.1\%, while binary detectors and membership inference methods achieve only 30--35\%.
6	
7	\paragraph{Accumulation settings.}
8	Our benchmark uses full-replacement chains; in practice, model training involves accumulation, mixing human data with synthetic outputs at unknown proportions \cite{gerstgrasser2024_accumulate}.
9	Under accumulation, depth signal should attenuate because each generation retains human-originated distributional properties, slowing tail erosion.
10	The 66.4\% cross-model and 59.1\% cross-strategy transfer should be interpreted as ceiling estimates for the mixed-data regime.
11	Extending the framework to accumulation, where depth becomes a continuous mixing parameter, is a priority for future work.
12	
13	\paragraph{Scale and deployment implications.}
14	The depth signal attenuates with model scale: at 14B, d0/d1 separability drops sharply ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$), though d1--d3 discrimination persists above chance.
15	This suggests that depth estimation complements rather than replaces binary detection for practical data auditing: binary detectors handle the human-vs-machine boundary where depth estimation is redundant ($>$97\% d0 recall), while depth estimation provides the finer ordinal resolution that binary methods cannot.
16	A practical deployment path proceeds from controlled settings, where model and strategy are known, to partially known settings via strategy-identification routing, to fully unknown settings requiring strategy-invariant features (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Open challenges.}
19	Cross-strategy transfer (d1--d3: 45.9\%) remains the primary bottleneck, driven by structurally distinct feature-space trajectories across decoding strategies.
20	Scale-dependent signal attenuation, depth-quality entanglement, and extension to accumulation settings are open challenges.
21	
```

      "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nThis paper formalizes recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification under controlled full-replacement chains.\nThe perplexity tail feature family dominates depth discrimination across all nine cells, with $\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}4$ for all three families (confirmed by 5-class extension, 71--80\\%; Appendix~\\ref{app:depth4_ext}); cross-model transfer reaches 66.4\\% (d1--d3: 55.4\\%), cross-domain arXiv 74.1\\%, while binary detectors and membership inference methods achieve only 30--35\\%.\n\n\\paragraph{Accumulation settings.}\nOur benchmark uses full-replacement chains; in practice, model training involves accumulation, mixing human data with synthetic outputs at unknown proportions \\cite{gerstgrasser2024_accumulate}.\nUnder accumulation, depth signal should attenuate because each generation retains human-originated distributional properties, slowing tail erosion.\nThe 66.4\\% cross-model and 59.1\\% cross-strategy transfer should be interpreted as ceiling estimates for the mixed-data regime.\nExtending the framework to accumulation, where depth becomes a continuous mixing parameter, is a priority for future work.\n\n\\paragraph{Scale and deployment implications.}\nThe depth signal attenuates with model scale: at 14B, d0/d1 separability drops sharply ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$), though d1--d3 discrimination persists above chance.\nThis suggests that depth estimation complements rather than replaces binary detection for practical data auditing: binary detectors handle the human-vs-machine boundary where depth estimation is redundant ($>$97\\% d0 recall), while depth estimation provides the finer ordinal resolution that binary methods cannot.\nA practical deployment path proceeds from controlled settings, where model and strategy are known, to partially known settings via strategy-identification routing, to fully unknown settings requiring strategy-invariant features (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}).\n\n\\paragraph{Open challenges.}\nCross-strategy transfer (d1--d3: 45.9\\%) remains the primary bottleneck, driven by structurally distinct feature-space trajectories across decoding strategies.\nScale-dependent signal attenuation, depth-quality entanglement, and extension to accumulation settings are open challenges.\n",
      "numLines": 21,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 21
    }
  },

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:426:Qwen2.5-14B$^\dagger$ & 0.643 & 0.713 & 0.728 & \multicolumn{2}{c}{--} \\
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:432:Among instruction-tuned models, Llama-3.1-8B exhibits monotonic AUC decay consistent with the 1--1.5B pattern, while Qwen2.5-7B shows a U-shaped recovery beyond d2v3 (Table~\ref{tab:cross_scale_auc}).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:433:A base-model control (Llama-3.1-8B without instruction tuning) clarifies the causal direction: the base model achieves 59.9\% four-class accuracy (95\% CI [57.8, 62.15], $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}2$) with non-monotonic AUC decay (Table~\ref{tab:llama3_base_auc}), while the instruction-tuned variant reaches 70.2\% with strictly monotonic decay.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:440:\label{tab:llama3_base_auc}

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:462:For Qwen2.5-7B, the CI lower bound (58.0\%) does not exclude sub-60\% accuracy; Llama-3.1-8B's lower bound (68.3\%) is more comfortably above chance but still below the 75--83\% range at 1--1.5B.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:463:The divergent AUC trajectories across two models caution against strong generalizations from only two 7--8B data points: both the monotonic (Llama-3.1) and non-monotonic (Qwen2.5) patterns are single observations, and further architectures are needed to establish which is the dominant regime at this scale.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Confirmed: `\cite{llama3}`, `\cite{qwen2.5}`, and `\cite{sugiyama2007_importance_weighting}` only appear in the drafts folder, NOT in the actual submitted paper tex files. These are orphan citations in references.bib - they're in the bibliography but not cited in the actual paper. R2's W8 claim is valid.

Now I have all the information I need. Let me write my cross-review response.

Let me organize my responses:

**W1 (Abstract 数字堆砌)**: I count the numbers in the abstract - indeed there are 20+ distinct numerical values. This is a valid writing concern. [AGREE]


**W2 (Discussion 过薄)**: The Discussion+Conclusion runs about 250 words across 20 lines, which feels sparse given the 9 experimental conditions and 8 generalization axes tested. The Extended Discussion in Appendix N has substantive content that could strengthen the main narrative, though I'd frame this as a Should-Fix rather than Must-Fix. [AGREE on the concern]
**W3 (方法层面无算法贡献)**: The authors are transparent about this being an empirical study, and ARR guidelines caution against penalizing papers that don't claim algorithmic novelty when they're positioned as empirical work. The classifier convergence finding is interesting but the thought cuts off here.

**W4 (Full-replacement only, lack of accumulation)**: This mirrors my earlier W8 concern. [AGREE]

**W5 (AI writing patterns)**: The -ing construction does appear frequently—20+ instances of "confirming/demonstrating/suggesting"—which is a fair stylistic observation. But ARR guidance prioritizes substance over presentation, so I'm treating this as minor. [AGREE as secondary]

**W6 (EDD limited novelty)**: The individual components are standard, yet their combination for this specific task constitutes the contribution. Many useful NLP metrics work this way—combining known pieces in novel configurations. [REBUT partially]

**W7 (14B confounds in Abstract)**: This was my Must-Fix W2, and I agree it needs addressing. [AGREE strongly]

**W8 (Orphan citations)**: The next thinking appears to be cut off here.

**W9 (Curation PoC multiple comparison)**: This aligns with my W4. [AGREE]

**Q1 (vs Broken Telephone)**: The Related Work section distinguishes this work from Mohamed et al.—they describe *how* degradation occurs without classifying *which* iteration, whereas this paper classifies the ordinal position. The distinction exists but could be stated more explicitly.

**Q2 (Cross-strategy inversion questions depth definition)**: Depth is defined procedurally—counting iterations—rather than by features. Strategy-dependent features don't undermine the definition itself; they affect how we estimate it. The two-stage pipeline proposal addresses this estimation challenge appropriately.

Continuing with Q3...

parameter counts alone don't account for the performance differences—OLMo 1B sometimes outperforms Pythia 1.4B, suggesting architecture and training data play larger roles. For Q4, the theoretical justification in section 3.6 about tail mass contraction provides some support for EDD=4, though with only three model families tested, this could easily be coincidental. Weighing the overall assessment, R2 scored 3.0 emphasizing limited algorithmic novelty and constrained EDD contributions, while I assessed 3.5 given the paper's explicit empirical framing. R2's concerns about abstract clarity and shallow discussion are legitimate points I underweighted, but I still lean toward 3.5 as the more appropriate score.

The core issues—reference model circularity, overstated cross-scale claims, and d0 inflation—are real problems but addressable through revision, and the empirical contribution remains solid enough for a borderline conference acceptance.
```

Classifier convergence（RF/XGBoost/LightGBM 在 0.4pp 内收敛，§5.1）本身是一个有价值的 empirical finding：它证明了信号瓶颈在特征设计而非分类器——这一结论指导了未来研究方向（改进特征而非改进分类器）。\n4. 类比：许多被广泛引用的 NLP benchmark 论文（如 GLUE、SuperGLUE、MMLU）的方法贡献也是 \"standard model + standard protocol\"，其价值在于 task formalization 和 systematic evaluation。\n5. R2 自己也在 S1 中认可了 \"novel and timely task formalization\"——如果任务形式化是核心贡献，那么用标准方法证明信号存在性正是合适的方法论选择。\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W4: Full-replacement only，缺乏 accumulation 实验**\n完全同意。我的审稿（R1-W8）也将此列为 Should-Fix。论文在 Discussion 中声称 full-replacement 结果是 accumulation 的 \"ceiling estimate\"（§6, para 2），但这一单调性假设缺乏理论或实验支持。即使是一个 toy-scale 实验（如 50% human + 50% synthetic 的 2-depth 混合链）也会大幅增强论文的生态效度。\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W5: -ing 短语 AI 写作痕迹**\n事实确认：我 grep 了全文，\", confirming/demonstrating/suggesting/indicating/revealing/establishing \" 模式在正文+附录中出现 **20 次**（仅 \"confirming that\" 就有 8 次），R2 的 \"9+ 次\" 是保守估计。这确实是显著的 AI 写作标志。但我将此列为 **Minor** 而非 Should-Fix：ARR guidelines 强调 \"assess substance over style\"，这些模式不影响论文的技术内容或可读性。修复也很简单（将 -ing 分词短语改写为独立句子）。\n\n**[REBUT] R2-W6: EDD 创新程度有限**\n部分不同意。R2 将 EDD 描述为 \"pairwise AUC + threshold + contiguous prefix\"，这在技术组件层面是准确的，但低估了其概念贡献：\n1. EDD 的价值不在于组件的技术新颖性，而在于**将 model collapse 理论的定性预测转化为可操作的定量指标**。这类 operationalization 在 NLP 中有悠久传统（BLEU = n-gram precision + brevity penalty；ROUGE = recall-based overlap；BERTScore = contextual embedding similarity——组件都不新，但组合后的 operationalization 有独立价值）。\n2. contiguous prefix 要求使 EDD 具有可解释的语义：\"前 d 个深度都可以可靠区分\"，比简单的 \"最大可区分对\" 更有实际部署意义。\n3. 不过，我在 R1-W6 中指出了 EDD 在非单调场景下的过度保守性（如 Qwen2.5-7B 的 U 型 AUC），论文没有提供替代指标。这是 EDD 定义的一个真实局限，但不等于 \"创新不足\"。\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W7: 14B 实验 5 个 confounds 在 Abstract 中的描述可能误导**\n强烈同意。我的审稿（R1-W2）将此列为 Must-Fix。Abstract 中 \"depth signal persists at larger scales\" 对 14B（EDD=0, d0v1 AUC=0.643）不成立。5 个混淆因素（QLoRA量化 / 500 samples / 单策略 / 词表不匹配 / self-reference）使 cross-scale 结论不可靠。R2 和我在这一点上完全一致。\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W8: 3 条孤儿引用**\n已验证：`llama3`、`qwen2.5`、`sugiyama2007_importance_weighting` 存在于 references.bib 中，但在实际提交的 tex 文件中**均未被 \\cite**（仅出现在 drafts/ 目录的未使用草稿中）。这是提交前清理的遗漏，属于 Minor 但应修复。\n\n**[AGREE] R2-W9: Curation PoC 未做 multiple comparison correction**\n同意，且我的审稿（R1-W4）对此有更详细的分析：rf_moderate vs random_moderate 的 p=0.020 在 Bonferroni 校正（α=0.017）下不通过，但 Appendix N 声称 \"2 of 3 surviving\"。R2 的 concern 与我一致。\n\n---\n\n### Questions 逐条回应\n\n**[REBUT] R2-Q1: 与 Mohamed et al. (2025) \"Broken Telephone\" 的区分**\nRelated Work §2（Adjacent Tasks 段）已明确区分：\"Mohamed et al. (2025) characterize *how* text degrades under iterative generation without classifying which iteration a passage comes from. Our task performs the inverse: ordinal depth estimation from text alone.\" 区分是清晰的：Mohamed et al. 分析退化现象（descriptive），本文估计迭代次数（predictive/classificatory）。不过，可以建议作者补充一句说明为什么 \"Broken Telephone\" 的 degradation characterization 不能直接用于深度分类（如：是否因为其特征不具有序数单调性？）。\n\n**[AGREE] R2-Q2: Cross-strategy d1/d2 inversion 是否质疑 depth 的 strategy-invariance**\n这是一个有深度的概念问题。我的回答是：它质疑的不是 **depth 定义的 strategy-invariance**（depth 是过程定义，策略无关），而是**基于当前特征集的 depth estimation 的 strategy-invariance**。温度采样加速收敛（§3.6），使得 temp d1 的统计特征类似于 nucleus d2——这是特征空间的对齐问题，不是深度定义的问题。但 R2 提出这个问题的价值在于：论文应更明确地区分 \"depth as a process property\"（strategy-invariant）和 \"depth estimation via perplexity features\"（strategy-dependent）。当前的 Discussion 没有充分展开这一区分，这也印证了 R2-W2（Discussion 过薄）的 concern。\n\n**[REBUT] R2-Q3: GPT-2 XL 最高准确率是 architecture 还是 parameter count**\n参数量不能解释准确率排序。OLMo-1B（1B 参数）在 temperature 策略下达到 82.3%，超过 Pythia-1.4B（1.4B）的 76.8%——如果参数量是主导因素，这一反转不应出现。更可能的解释是训练数据差异：GPT-2 XL（WebText）的训练数据与 Wikipedia seed 的分布差异可能产生了更强的深度信号（cross-distribution feature extraction）。这一点可以通过 Table 8（Appendix H）的 reference model ablation 间接验证：当 Pythia 数据使用 GPT-2 XL 作为参考模型时准确率**最低**（67.3%），而 GPT-2 XL 数据使用 Pythia 作为参考模型时准确率**最高**（85.9%）——这暗示 generator 与 reference 的分布差异本身可能放大了深度信号，值得进一步调查。\n\n**[AGREE] R2-Q4: EDD=4 跨三个 family 的一致性有无理论基础**\n§3.6 提供了部分理论基础（tail mass 以 α^k 收缩，α 为 contraction factor），如果三个模型的 α 在 1-1.5B 尺度下相近，EDD 收敛是可预期的。但仅 3 个模型族构不成统计意义上的 \"一致性\"——这可能是 scale 约束下的巧合。论文将 \"EDD_{0.70}=4 for all three families\" 写入 abstract 和 contribution 列表，但没有提供理论预测或 bootstrap 检验来支持这一一致性的显著性。R2 的质疑合理。\n\n---\n\n### 对 R2 Overall Assessment (3.0, Findings) 的评价\n\n**我不完全认同将 overall 降至 3.0。** 我的评分为 3.5（Borderline Conference），与 R2 的核心分歧在 W3（方法无算法贡献）和 W6（EDD 创新有限）：\n\n1. **W3 的权重分歧**：R2 将 \"RF + 已知特征\" 视为 Should-Fix 级别的弱点，但 ARR guidelines 明确警告不要惩罚 empirical contribution 论文缺乏算法创新。论文的价值在于：(a) 定义了一个被忽视但重要的新任务；(b) 通过系统实验证明了信号的存在性和特征结构；(c) 证明了分类器不是瓶颈。这种 \"task + benchmark + systematic empirical characterization\" 的贡献模式在 *ACL venues 中有清晰的先例。\n\n2. **W6 与 W3 的耦合**：如果因为 RF 不新（W3）又因为 EDD 不新（W6），论文的方法贡献就被双重折扣了。但 EDD 的贡献是 operationalization 而非 algorithmic novelty，与 W3 属于不同维度。\n\n3. **R2 与 R1 的共识区域**：我们在以下问题上完全一致——full-replacement scope limitation（R2-W4 / R1-W8）、cross-scale confounds（R2-W7 / R1-W2）、curation PoC 不显著（R2-W9 / R1-W4）、Abstract 需要改善（R2-W1 / R1-W3 部分重叠）。这些问题是 **可修复的**，不构成 fundamental flaw。\n\n4. **R2 比 R1 更敏感的区域**：Discussion 厚度（W2）和 AI 写作痕迹（W5）。这些是合理的关注，但它们的修复成本低（增写 Discussion + 改写 -ing 短语），不应将论文从 Conference 边缘推到 Findings。\n\n5. **R1 比 R2 更敏感的区域**：Reference model circularity（R1-W1）、feature importance 在高共线性下的过度解释（R1-W5）、cross-seed 覆盖不足（R1-W7）。这些是 R2 未覆盖的方法严谨性问题，但同样可修复。\n\n**综合判断**：论文的 Must-Fix 问题（reference model claim 矛盾、cross-scale claim 校准、d0 inflation 前置）是 claim-strength 校准问题，而非 fundamental methodological flaw。在 claim 校准后，任务形式化 + 系统实验 + EDD 指标 + 特征一致性发现的组合贡献足以支撑 Borderline Conference（3.5），前提是作者在 rebuttal 中充分回应 W1-W3 的修复方案。如果 rebuttal 不能令人信服地回应 reference model circularity 和 cross-scale confounds，我会降至 3.0。",
  "reviewer_id": "1",
  "project": "recursive_gen_depth_est"
}
